def fft_convolve(a,b):
 n=a.size+b.size-1
 nfft=1 << (n-1).bit_length()
 fa=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,nfft)
 c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,nfft)[:n]
 return c

Should we use scipy.signal.fftconvolve? It may pad automatically and be slower due overhead. Custom with precomputed nfft maybe faster. We can define fast_convolve:
def fast_convolution(a,b=None):
    n=len(a)
    if b is None: b=a
    if n < 128: return np.convolve(a,b)
    out_len=n+len(b)-1
    nfft=1 << (out_len-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
    fb=np.fft.rfft(b,nfft) if b is not a else fa
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,nfft)[:out_len]
But repeated nfft. Could cache by length global dict? Top-level cache dict.

L-BFGS call:
def run_lbfgs_init(a_init, max_time):
    best_a=a_init; best_score=fast_score(a_init)
    x=np.log(np.maximum(a_init,1e-30))
    tau_s=[0.5,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005]
    for tau_factor in tau_s:
       tau=tau_factor/N
       define obj(x): return smooth_obj(x,tau)
       define jac(x): return smooth_grad(x,tau)
       res=minimize(obj,x,jac,method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': max(200, int(max_time/50)?), 'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-12,'maxfun': int(max_time*20)?})
       after res update best if score better.
    return best.

Then line search:
def projected_step(a, grad, step):
    v = a - step*grad
    v = np.maximum(v,0)
    s=v.sum()
    if s<eps: return a
    return v/s

Let's inspect previous state-of-art text: It normalizes sum=√(2n), then LP maximizing sum g subject to f*g≤M and g≥0. This LP step has a nice interpretation: For a fixed f and M, the best g in the tangent cone of KKT. It may escape local minima because linearizes convex? Maybe implementing it with an active set and solving a small LP via scipy.optimize.linprog could improve. We can approximate the LP by solving for g via projected subgradient on the dual? Let's examine.
LP primal: variables g (n vars nonnegative). Constraints: for k=0..2n-2, A_k g ≤ M. A_{k,j}=f_{k-j} if valid else 0. Objective maximize 1^T g. This is a linear program in n with 2n-1 constraints. Because A is convolution matrix (circulant-like) and M scalar. The dual: minimize M * 1^T y subject to A^T y ≥ 1, y≥0. A^T y length n is (f*y)_i (convolution length?). This dual has 2n-1 variables. We need the maximum sum g. This LP is feasible? If f sums √(2n), M=||f*f||∞. Uniform f? The LP optimum maybe gives direction.
Can we solve dual with a few iterations? Constraint (f*y)_i ≥1. This is like find nonnegative y minimizing M sum y subject to lower bounds on convolution with f. Since f*y is convolution. This is a covering problem. It can be solved by multiplicative weights: start y uniform, increase where (f*y)_i <1. But y is nonnegative continuous; the objective is linear. We can use a simple coordinate ascent/subgradient? The dual min over y≥0 of M∑y s.t. f*y ≥1. This is an interval covering with weights f. Since f positive, feasible. We can solve approximately using projected gradient? But variable count same. However, we can exploit f maybe smooth and use FFT.
Alternatively solve primal directly by setting g as proportional to a dual slack? The LP optimum can be found if we solve a small system on active constraints? The SOTA says optimum at extreme point determined by active set. We could iteratively maintain active constraints and solve equality? Hard.

Maybe use `scipy.optimize.linprog` with method='highs' if available? n=4806, constraints 9611. Matrix dense? coefficient for row k is f[j] for j in window; trapezoid, about 23M nonzeros. scipy linprog HiGHS can handle 23M dense? Might be memory/time huge. Sparse A: use scipy.sparse lil. Each row has up to n nonzeros; total ~23M, sparse matrix memory ~23M*8=184MB plus overhead, too high. But maybe N=1024 for LP search; then update. Could use smaller n to find shape. But not likely faster.

We can implement a greedy primal heuristic for g: start g=uniform, constraints A g >M. Need reduce g where constraints binding? To maximize sum under A g≤M, it's like water filling. Use multiplicative weights on primal? For constraints violation v_k = A_k g / M. If v_k>1, reduce g_j for j in row with weights f_{k-j}? This is similar. Could alternate: g ← normalize(g * exp(-η * (A g - M?)_indicator? )). Specifically, for row k violation, decrease each g_j proportionally to contribution f_{k-j}. Update g_j = g_j * exp(-η ∑_{k: violation} w_{k,j}) where w_{k,j}=max(0,A_k g - M? or f_{k-j}). This is like cutting. It can produce a feasible direction. Similar to previous subgradient but for dual? Could help.

Maybe simpler: Instead of LP, use a multiplicative Weights update directly on a: For each active lag k, gradient = 2 shift(a,k). We already do.

Param search details:
```
def random_param_inits(N, num, rng, time_limit):
    inits=[]; scores=[]
    x=np.linspace(0,1,N)
    base candidates...
    for t in range(num):
       a,b,c = rng.uniform(-3,3,3)
       vals=np.exp(a*x + b*x*x + c*x*x*x)
       if vals.max()>1e6: vals=np.log(vals)-max? Use exp of shifted.
       vals = np.maximum(vals,1e-300)
       a_seq=vals/vals.sum()
       s=fast_score(a_seq)
       update best
    # after random, Nelder-Mead on best theta for exponential degree3:
       minimize exp_objective(theta,N)
    return inits
```

def correlate_weights_a(w,a):
    w=np.asarray(w); a=np.asarray(a); nw=w.size; na=a.size
    if nw*na <= 200000: # direct maybe
       # w length>=na; use loops? Better direct via np.convolve for small.

    L=_next_pow2(nw+na-1)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,L); fa=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],L)

```

def subgradient_polish(a, max_time):
    best_score=fast_score(a); best_a=a.copy(); a=a.copy()
    step=1e-4 # normalized grad scale max1
    no_improve=10
    deadline=time.monotonic()+max_time
    K=400
    last_smooth=0
    while time < deadline:
       c=fast_convolution(a); M=c.max()
       # active top K, maybe with threshold
       idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]
       w=np.zeros_like(c); w[idx]=1.0
       h=correlate_weights_a(w,a)
       g=2*h/K
       g -= np.mean(g)
       gmax=np.max(np.abs(g)); if gmax<=0: break
       g = g/gmax
       # try step and maybe varied
       best_this=best_score? Use current score = fast_score(a) maybe.
       cur=fast_score(a)
       found=False
       for scale in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.12,0.06,0.03,0.015,0.007]:
           v=a - (step*scale)*g
           v=np.maximum(v,0); s=v.sum(); if s<eps: continue
           v/=s
           sc=fast_score(v)
           if sc < cur - 1e-12:
               a=v; cur=sc; step*=2? maybe cap; found=True; break
           # else keep v? no
       if found:
           if cur < best_score: best_score=cur; best_a=a.copy()
           no_improve=0
           if step < 0.5: step *=1.5
       else:
           no_improve +=1
           step *=0.7
           if no_improve > 8:
              # reset to best, maybe random perturbation
              a=best_a.copy(); cur=best_score
              step=1e-4
              for _ in range(3): perturb...
              if no improve break?
       # every 50 iterations, run a quick LSE step? maybe not.
    return best_a,best_score
```

def run_lse_lbfgs(a_init, max_time):
   n=len(a_init)
   best_a=a_init.copy(); best_score=fast_score(a_init)
   x=np.log(np.maximum(a_init,1e-30))
   tau_factors = [1.5, 0.8, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.025, 0.012, 0.006]
   # if max_time > 400 add more
   for factor in tau_factors:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline or max_time <= 30: break
       tau=factor/n
       _CBC? call minimize with options.
       # set maxiter based on factor: early more? maxiter=600, maxfun=1500.
       res = minimize(lse_obj_args, x, args=(tau,), jac=lse_grad_args, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxiter': 800, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxfun': 2000, 'maxls':50})
       if res.status? no matter.
       x=res.x
       a=softmax(x)
       s=fast_score(a)
       if s<best_score: update
       # if s not improved from start of stage and factor small, maybe continue.
   return best_a,best_score
```

We can find P via iterative square root in z-domain: solve polynomial p of degree n-1 such that p(z)^2 approx desired q(z). This is like finding a nonnegative square root of a polynomial. If q is a "valid" autocorrelation? But convolution not autocorrelation. For any polynomial q with q(0)>0, there is a formal power series square root p = sqrt(q) in z. We can truncate to degree n-1. Let's formalize. Given a target nonnegative cost sequence c[0..2n-2] (desired convolution), Newton iteration for square root p of c (as polynomial) truncated to n:
```
p = np.zeros(n); p[0]=sqrt(c[0])
for k in 1..n-1:
   # c_k = 2 p0 p_k + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-i} (if c known)
   p_k = (c_k - sum_{i=1}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-i})/(2 p0)
```

How to compute root efficiently in top-level:
```
def square_root_sequence(target, max_len):
    # target length >= max_len, nonnegative, target[0]>0
    n=max_len
    p=np.zeros(n)
    p0 = np.sqrt(max(target[0],0))
    if p0 <= 0: return None
    p[0]=p0
    for k in range(1,n):
        s=0
        prev=p[:k]
        # sum_{i=0}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-1-i}? Actually recurrence: target[k] = 2 p0 p_k + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-i} + p_{k-1} p_1? This sum over i=1..k-1 p_i p_{k-i}. Equivalent sum_{i=0}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-1-i} - p0 p_{k-1}? Wait conventional convolution c_k=∑_{i=0}^k p_i p_{k-i}. Terms: p0 p_k + p_k p0 + ∑_{i=1}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-i}. So c_k = 2 p0 p_k + conv_prev(k) where conv_prev(k)=∑_{i=1}^{k-1}... = ∑_{i=0}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-1-i} - p0 p_{k-1}? Actually ∑_{i=0}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-1-i} is coefficient k-1 of p*p (without p_k absent). It includes p0 p_{k-1}+p_{k-1}p0? Let's test k=2: need p1^1? c2=2p0p2+p1^2. conv_prev coeff for k-1=1: p0p1+p1p0=2p0p1. Not. So direct dot p[1:k] with p[k-2::-1]? For k=2, p[1:2] dot p[0]? no.
Use `np.dot(p[1:k], p[k-2::-1])`? For k=3: i=1..1 p1 p1; p[1:3]=[p1,p2? p2 unknown] not.
Need sum i=1 to k-1 p_i p_{k-i}. Here indices up to k-1. For k=5: i=1..4 p1p4+p2p3+p3p2+p4p1, but p4 unknown if k=5? Wait p_{k-i}, for i=1 gives p4, unknown. Ah recurrence for p_k uses p_{k-i} where i from1 to k-1; if i<k, p_{k-i} includes p_{k-1} down to p_1, all known, not p_k. For k=5, terms p1p4 (known p4), p2p3,p3p2,p4p1; yes p4 known. Good. So sum can be computed as ∑_{i=1}^{k-1} p_i p_{k-i}. This is convolution coefficient k excluding p0p_k and p_kp0. It equals full convolution coefficient k of p_{0..k-1} with p_{0..?}. We can maintain full convolution incrementally? But O(k) per step with vectorized dot:
```
s = np.dot(p[1:k], p[k-1:0:-1])? For k=3: p[1:3]=[p1,p2? p2 unknown! Wait p[k-1:0:-1] includes p2 if k=3? k-1=2, down to1 gives [p2,p1], p2 unknown at step 2? def make_flat_root_init(n, offset=0, peak=0):
   c = np.ones(n)
   # add central bump for last coefficients: c[-m:] *= 1+peak
   p = formal_square_root(c)
   if p is None: return None
   p = np.maximum(p,0)
   psum=p.sum()
   if psum<1e-12: return None
   return p/psum
```

Implement formal root:
```
def formal_square_root(c, n=None):
    if n is None: n=len(c)
    c=np.asarray(c,dtype=np.float64)
    if c[0] <= 0: return None
    p=np.zeros(n)
    p[0]=np.sqrt(c[0])
    inv=1.0/(2*p[0])
    # Need c length at least n; if c shorter repeat last? target half length n.
    for k in range(1,n):
        if k >= len(c): ck = c[-1]  # or 0
        else: ck = c[k]
        if k > 1:
            # dot p[1:k] with p[1:k][::-1]
            seg = p[1:k]
            s = np.dot(seg, seg[::-1])
        else:
            s = 0.0
        pk = (ck - s) * inv
        # if pk negative small, allow? keep for recurrence? If we clip during generation, root changes. Maybe keep negative if small.
        p[k]=pk
    return p
```
For k=1: c1 = 2 p0 p1, s=0. Works. For k=2: c2=2p0p2+p1^2, s=p1^2. Good. If p becomes negative, can still continue but final nonneg fail. We can if pk < -tol maybe return None; if small negative set 0? But setting 0 changes recurrence for later; maybe okay for heuristic. Use `if pk < -1e-12 * max(1,abs(ck)): return None`? For target shapes maybe positive. If small negative due numerical, set 0.
But if we set pk=0, subsequent s changes? It's okay with p already set. We can set lower bound 0 at each step to enforce p nonnegative while still approximate root. This makes recurrence a constrained square root; should be fine.

Let's refine subgradient line search to be more powerful:
- Compute c and active weights w (smooth tau small). Compute gradient direction in log-space: d = a*(g - dot) with g=grad_a. Since log update a_new = a * exp(λ d) approximates a + λ a d. Project positive and normalize. This can move entries more proportionally, better for simplex. We can line search over λ in log space.
   Let x=log(a); gradient_x = a*(grad_a - dot). For step, a_new = softmax(x + λ grad_x) (exact) or exp. Use λ small. This is L-BFGS step direction. We can do simple gradient descent in log with line search and adaptive tau. This may be easier and avoid L-BFGS memory. But L-BFGS accelerates.

def make_root_target(n, kind, p): # kind 0 uniform,1 increasing,2 endbump,3 osc
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   if kind==0: c=1
   elif kind==1: c=1+p0*t**p1
   elif kind==2: c=np.exp(p0*np.exp(-((t-1)**2)/p1^2))? p1 maybe.
   return c
```

```
def singular_init(n, alpha, beta, side):
  i = np.arange(n)
  eps=0.5
  if side==0: base=(i/n+eps)^(-alpha) * (1+beta*i/n)
  else: base=(1-i/n+eps)^(-alpha)*(1+beta*i/n)
```

`run_subgradient(a, max_time)`:
```
best_score=fast_score(a); best=a.copy()
step=1e-4; K=max(200, len(c)//8); no_improve=0; it=0
while time<deadline:
   it+=1
   c=fft_convolve_self(a); M=c.max()
   # active weights: use top K but weighted by exp((c-M)/tau_P?) Could use top only.
   idx=np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]
   w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[idx]=1.0
   h=correlate_weights_a(w,a)
   g=2*h/K
   g -= np.mean(g)
   gmax=np.max(np.abs(g)); if gmax==0: break
   g/=gmax
   cur=fast_score(a)
   found=False
   # try current step and some variations
   for mult in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.12,0.06,0.03,0.015,0.007]:
       v=a - step*mult*g
       v=np.maximum(v,0)
       s=v.sum()
       if s<=0: continue
       v/=s
       sc=fast_score(v)
       if sc < cur:
          a=v; cur=sc; found=True; break
   if found:
      if cur < best_score:
          best_score=cur; best=a.copy()
      no_improve=0
      if step < 0.05: step*=1.3
   else:
      no_improve +=1
      step *= 0.7
      if no_improve > 10:
         # reset to best and try a smooth log direction
         a=best.copy(); cur=best_score
         step=1e-4; no_improve=0
         # maybe random perturb best a few times
         for _ in range(5):
             v=a + rng.normal(0, 1/(100*a.size), a.size)
             v=np.maximum(v,0); v/=v.sum(); sc=fast_score(v)
             if sc<best_score: best=a; best_score=sc; a=v; break
         if no random help and step<1e-8: break
   # every 100 it, do a tau-smooth gradient step? Could implement.
return best, best_score
```
Step normalized g max1. For N=4806, step 1e-4 may be too small? If no improve due below noise, reduce. Could increase if improve. Maybe start 5e-4. Use `step=1e-3`? Changing max entry by 1e-3 (5x mean) may be beneficial. Use line search mult includes down to small. If no improve, step reduces. If improve, increases up to 0.1. So starting 1e-3 okay. But first step might overshoot; mult can choose 0.12 etc. Good.

Implement:
```
def compute_lse_info(a, tau):
    c = fft_convolve_self(a)
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M <= 0:
       w=np.zeros_like(c); if c.size: w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
       return 0.0, 0.0, w
    shifted=c-M
    if tau <= 1e-15:
       idx=int(np.argmax(c)); w=np.zeros_like(c); w[idx]=1; return M,M,w
    e=np.exp(shifted/tau)
    s=e.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       w=zeros; w[argmax]=1; val=M
    else:
       w=e/s; val=M + tau*np.log(s)
    return val,M,w
```

def root_target_mods(base):
   b=np.asarray(base,float); b=np.maximum(b,1e-12); b/=b.sum()
   c=fft_convolve_self(b); first=c[:n]

Let's detail `run_lse_lbfgs` code:
```
def run_lse_lbfgs(a_init, max_time):
    a_init = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64)
    a_init = np.maximum(a_init, 0.0)
    s=a_init.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return a_init, np.inf
    a_init /= s
    best_a = a_init.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a_init)
    x = np.log(np.maximum(a_init, 1e-300))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max_time
    n = a_init.size
    tau_units = [1.5, 0.8, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.025, 0.012]
    if max_time > 300:
        tau_units = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03, 0.015, 0.008]
    for unit in tau_units:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline or max_time_remaining < 30: break
        tau = unit / n
        if tau <= 0: tau=1e-12
        maxiter = 400 if max_time > 400 else 250
        try:
            res = minimize(lse_obj_args, x, args=(tau,), jac=lse_grad_args, method='L-BFGS-B',
                           options={'maxiter': maxiter, 'ftol':1e-12, 'gtol':1e-8, 'maxfun': 2*maxiter, 'maxls':50})
            x = res.x
        except Exception:
            break
        a = softmax(x)
        sc = fast_score(a)
        if sc < best_score:
            best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy()
            print(f"  LSE tau={unit}: {best_score:.6f}")
    return best_a, best_score
```
`max_time_remaining` compute.

Could L-BFGS with tau very small return x causing softmax collapse; if score worse, best kept. Next stage x maybe collapsed; maybe not good. We can if score worse than previous best by >0.1, reset x to log(best_a) before next smaller tau? That might prevent moving away. Maybe:
```
if sc > best_score + 0.05: x = np.log(max(best_a,eps))
```
But stages may need explore. Use if no improvement. Keep.

def softmax(x):
   x=np.asarray(x,float)
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return np.ones(size)/size
   m=x.max(); if not finite: ...
   e=np.exp(x-m); s=e.sum(); if s==0: uniform; return e/s
```

`formal_square_root`: target c length n? If shorter, repeat last? We'll pass length n.
```
def formal_square_root(c, n):
  c=np.asarray(c,float); if c.size<n: extend with c[-1]
  if c[0] <= 0: c=c.copy(); c[0]=max(c[0],1e-15)
  p=np.zeros(n); p0=np.sqrt(c[0]); p[0]=p0; inv=1/(2*p0)
  for k in range(1,n):
      ck=c[k]
      if k>1:
         seg=p[1:k]
         s=np.dot(seg, seg[::-1])
      else: s=0.0
      pk=(ck - s)*inv
      if not np.isfinite(pk): return None
      if pk < 0:
         if pk < -1e-8 * max(1.0, abs(ck)):
             # maybe allow small by clipping
             pk=0.0
         pk=0.0
      p[k]=pk
  # if many negatives clipped, still ok
  if p.sum()<=0 or not finite: return None
  return p
```
If we set pk=0 after large negative, subsequent recurrence uses clipped p (already set), okay. But if c large, pk negative due target invalid; sequence might odd. Add candidate with clip.

Target mods: Use `first = np.maximum(first, 1e-12)`. median.

Add candidate function:
```
def add_candidate(cands, arr, max_keep):
   if arr is None: return
   a=np.asarray(arr,float)
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<=1e-12: return
   a=a/s
   # clean nonfinite
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return
   sc=fast_score(a)
   if not np.isfinite(sc): return
   cands.append((sc,a.copy()))
   if len(cands)>max_keep:
      worst=0
      for i in range(1,len(cands)):
         if cands[i][0] > cands[worst][0]: worst=i
      del cands[worst]
```
No lambda.

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative step-sequence minimizing:
    2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

The search combines:
  - fast FFT-based convolutions and scores,
  - several low-dimensional analytic inits (singular, affine, exponential, formal square-root inits),
  - root-of-first-half-convolution reparameterization to create new inits near a good incumbent,
  - L-BFGS on a log-sum-exp smoothing of the max-convolution,
  - exact projected subgradient line-search polishing.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

_FFT_CACHE = {}
_X_CACHE = {}


def _next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (int(m) - 1).bit_length()


def get_xgrid(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n not in _X_CACHE:
        _X_CACHE[n] = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return _X_CACHE[n]


def fft_convolve_self(a):
    """Full linear convolution a*a using FFT for larger sizes."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)

    out_len = 2 * n - 1
    if n < 96:
        return np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")

    L = _next_pow2(out_len)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    if c.size < out_len:
        c = np.concatenate([c, np.zeros(out_len - c.size)])
    c = c[:out_len]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def correlate_weights_a(w, a):
    """
    Compute h_i = sum_j w_{i+j} a_j for i=0..n-1,
    where len(w)=2n-1 and len(a)=n.
    """
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    nw = w.size
    na = a.size
    if na == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if nw < na:
        w = np.pad(w, (0, na - nw))
        nw = w.size

    # Direct for small cases.
    if nw * na <= 2000000:
        full = np.convolve(w, a[::-1], mode="full")
        return full[na - 1:na - 1 + na].copy()

    out_len = nw + na - 1
    L = _next_pow2(out_len)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    far = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], L)
    full = np.fft.irfft(fw * far, L)
    if full.size < na + na - 1:
        full = np.concatenate([full, np.zeros(na + na - 1 - full.size)])
    return full[na - 1:na - 1 + na]


def fast_score(a):
    """Fast evaluation of 2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    c = fft_convolve_self(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        if not np.isfinite(m):
            return float("inf")
        m = 0.0
    if s < 1e-6:
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def softmax(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    m = float(np.max(x))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    e = np.exp(x - m)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    return e / s


def compute_lse_info(a, tau):
    """Return (lse_value, max_c, normalized_weights)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    c = fft_convolve_self(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return 0.0, 0.0, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[0] = 1.0
        return 0.0, 0.0, w

    tau = float(tau)
    if not np.isfinite(tau) or tau <= 1e-15:
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return m, m, w

    shifted = c - m
    e = np.exp(shifted / tau)
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return m, m, w

    w = e / s
    val = m + tau * float(np.log(s))
    return val, m, w


def lse_obj_args(x, tau):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return 1e9
    a = softmax(x)
    val, _, _ = compute_lse_info(a, tau)
    if not np.isfinite(val):
        return 1e9
    return float(val)


def lse_grad_args(x, tau):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    a = softmax(x)
    _, _, w = compute_lse_info(a, tau)
    if w.size != 2 * n - 1:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    h = correlate_weights_a(w, a)
    ga = 2.0 * h
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(ga)):
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    dot = float(np.dot(a, ga))
    gx = a * (ga - dot)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(gx)):
        gx = np.nan_to_num(gx, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return gx


def run_lse_lbfgs(a_init, max_time):
    """Smoothed LSE + L-BFGS in log-simplex parameterization."""
    a = np.asarray(a_init, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        n = a.size
        a = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
        s = 1.0
    a = a / s
    n = a.size

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a)

    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-300))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max_time

    if max_time > 500:
        tau_units = [2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03, 0.015, 0.008]
    elif max_time > 250:
        tau_units = [1.5, 0.8, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.025, 0.012]
    else:
        tau_units = [1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03]

    if max_time > 500:
        maxiter = 450
    elif max_time > 250:
        maxiter = 350
    else:
        maxiter = 220

    for unit in tau_units:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline or (deadline - now) < 45:
            break
        tau = max(1e-12, unit / max(1, n))

        try:
            res = minimize(
                lse_obj_args,
                x,
                args=(tau,),
                jac=lse_grad_args,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                options={
                    "maxiter": maxiter,
                    "maxfun": 2 * maxiter,
                    "ftol": 1e-12,
                    "gtol": 1e-8,
                    "maxls": 50,
                },
            )
            x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        except Exception:
            break

        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            x = np.log(np.maximum(best_a, 1e-300))
            continue

        a2 = softmax(x)
        sc2 = fast_score(a2)
        if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
            best_score = sc2
            best_a = a2.copy()
            print(f"    LSE tau_unit={unit:.4f} score={best_score:.7f}")

    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # A final exact re-normalized score check.
        final_a = np.maximum(best_a, 0.0)
        fs = float(np.sum(final_a))
        if fs > 1e-12:
            final_a = final_a / fs
            scf = fast_score(final_a)
            if np.isfinite(scf) and scf < best_score:
                best_score = scf
                best_a = final_a.copy()

    return best_a, best_score


def project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return None
    return v / s


def subgradient_polish(a, max_time, rng):
    """Projected subgradient line search using active convolution peaks."""
    a = np.maximum(na := np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64), 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        a = np.ones_like(a) / max(1, a.size)
    else:
        a = a / s

    n = a.size
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a)
    cur_score = best_score

    deadline = time.monotonic() + max_time
    k = min(max(200, (2 * n - 1) // 8), 1600)
    step = 5.0e-4
    no_improve = 0
    iter_count = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter_count += 1
        c = fft_convolve_self(a)
        if c.size == 0:
            break

        # Active set: top convex peaks.
        order = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:k]
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        w[order] = 1.0

        h = correlate_weights_a(w, a)
        g = (2.0 / max(1, k)) * h
        g = g - float(np.mean(g))

        gscale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if gscale <= 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(gscale):
            break
        g = g / gscale
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

        found = False
        for mult in (1.0, 0.7, 0.45, 0.25, 0.12, 0.06, 0.03, 0.015, 0.007):
            v = project_simplex(a - step * mult * g)
            if v is None:
                continue
            sc = fast_score(v)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc + 1e-12 < cur_score:
                a = v
                cur_score = sc
                found = True
                break

        if found:
            if cur_score < best_score:
                best_score = cur_score
                best_a = a.copy()
            no_improve = 0
            if step < 2.0e-2:
                step *= 1.28
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            step *= 0.72
            if step < 1e-9:
                step = 1e-9

            if no_improve >= 12:
                a = best_a.copy()
                cur_score = best_score
                step = 5.0e-4
                no_improve = 0

                # Small random kicks, only if they help.
                for _ in range(8):
                    noise = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0 / (30.0 * n), n)
                    kv = project_simplex(best_a + noise)
                    if kv is None:
                        continue
                    ksc = fast_score(kv)
                    if np.isfinite(ksc) and ksc < best_score:
                        best_a = kv.copy()
                        best_score = ksc
                        a = kv.copy()
                        cur_score = ksc
                        break

        if iter_count % 100 == 0:
            print(f"    polish iter={iter_count} best={best_score:.7f} cur={cur_score:.7f} step={step:.3e}")

    return best_a, best_score


def same_array(a, b):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size != b.size:
        return False
    return float(np.max(np.abs(a - b))) < 1e-12


def make_linear_shape(n, c):
    x = get_xgrid(n)
    v = 1.0 + c * (2.0 * x - 1.0)
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-12)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return v / s


def make_singular_shape(n, alpha, beta, side):
    """
    side=0: high near the left edge, scaled by alpha.
    side=1: high near the right edge.
    """
    i = np.arange(n)
    t = (i + 0.5) / float(n)
    eps = 1e-12
    if side == 0:
        base = np.power(t + eps, -alpha)
    else:
        base = np.power(1.0 - t + eps, -alpha)
    v = base * (1.0 + beta * t)
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-12)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return v / s


def make_exp_poly_shape(n, theta):
    x = get_xgrid(n)
    if theta is None or len(theta) < 2:
        return None
    a = float(theta[0])
    b = float(theta[1])
    c = float(theta[2]) if len(theta) > 2 else 0.0
    z = a * x + b * x * x + c * x * x * x
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        return None
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    v = np.exp(z)
    v = np.maximum(v, 1e-300)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return v / s


def exp_poly_score_args(theta, n):
    a = make_exp_poly_shape(n, theta)
    if a is None:
        return 1e8
    sc = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return 1e8
    return float(sc)


def refine_exp_theta(theta0, n, max_iter=900):
    theta0 = np.asarray(theta0, dtype=np.float64)
    try:
        res = minimize(
            exp_poly_score_args,
            theta0,
            args=(n,),
            method="Nelder-Mead",
            options={"maxiter": max_iter, "xatol": 1e-5, "fatol": 1e-9},
        )
        return np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64), float(res.fun)
    except Exception:
        return theta0, fast_score(make_exp_poly_shape(n, theta0))


def add_candidate(cands, arr, max_keep):
    if arr is None:
        return
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return
    a = a / s
    sc = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(sc):
        return
    cands.append((float(sc), a.copy()))
    if len(cands) > max_keep:
        worst = 0
        for i in range(1, len(cands)):
            if cands[i][0] > cands[worst][0]:
                worst = i
        del cands[worst]


def formal_square_root(target, n):
    """
    Formal causal square root p of a one-sided polynomial target:
      P(z)^2 = Target(z) modulo z^n.
    Positive parts are kept; negative recurrence values are clipped to zero.
    """
    target = np.asarray(target, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if target.size < n:
        pad = float(target[-1]) if target.size else 1.0
        target = np.concatenate([target, np.full(n - target.size, pad)])
    target = target[:n]
    target = np.maximum(target, 1e-15)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(target)):
        return None

    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p0 = float(np.sqrt(target[0]))
    if p0 <= 1e-150 or not np.isfinite(p0):
        return None
    p[0] = p0
    inv2p0 = 1.0 / (2.0 * p0)

    for k in range(1, n):
        ck = float(target[k])
        if k == 1:
            s = 0.0
        else:
            seg = p[1:k]
            s = float(np.dot(seg, seg[::-1]))
            if not np.isfinite(s):
                return None
        pk = (ck - s) * inv2p0
        if not np.isfinite(pk):
            return None
        if pk < 0.0:
            pk = 0.0
        p[k] = pk

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return None
    ps = float(np.sum(p))
    if ps <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return p


def generate_root_mod_inits(base, cands, max_keep):
    """Reconstruct sequences from smoothed/capped first-half convolutions."""
    b = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size == 0:
        return
    n = b.size
    b = np.maximum(b, 1e-12)
    bs = float(np.sum(b))
    if bs <= 1e-12:
        return
    b = b / bs

    c = fft_convolve_self(b)
    if c.size < n:
        return
    first = np.maximum(c[:n].copy(), 1e-12)
    med = float(np.median(first))
    if med <= 1e-12:
        med = 1e-12

    # Blend toward median.
    for eta in (0.10, 0.25, 0.50, 0.75):
        targ = (1.0 - eta) * first + eta * med
        p = formal_square_root(targ, n)
        add_candidate(cands, p, max_keep)

    # Cap upper tail of first half.
    for q in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.50, 1.00):
        cap = med * (1.0 + q)
        if cap <= 1e-12:
            cap = med
        targ = np.minimum(first.copy(), cap)
        targ = np.maximum(targ, 1e-12)
        p = formal_square_root(targ, n)
        add_candidate(cands, p, max_keep)


def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    """Main search entry point."""
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0
    budget_s = max(60.0, min(budget_s, 2400.0))

    hard_end = time.monotonic() + budget_s - 35.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(24601)

    # Choose final length. Prefer the supplied construction if reasonable.
    N = 4806
    global_arr = None
    if "height_sequence_1" in globals():
        try:
            g = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
            g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
            gs = float(np.sum(g))
            if g.size >= 512 and g.size <= 9000 and gs > 1e-12 and np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
                N = int(g.size)
                global_arr = g / gs
        except Exception:
            pass

    if N < 256:
        N = 256
    if N > 9000:
        N = 4806

    candidates = []
    max_candidates = 22

    # Best known incumbent.
    if global_arr is not None:
        add_candidate(candidates, global_arr, max_candidates)

    # Simple analytical inits.
    add_candidate(candidates, np.ones(N), max_candidates)

    for cval in (-0.45, -0.30, -0.15, 0.0, 0.15, 0.30, 0.45, 0.49):
        add_candidate(candidates, make_linear_shape(N, cval), max_candidates)

    # Singular / weakly singular edges often help lower the convolution peak.
    for alpha in (0.25, 0.35, 0.40, 0.45, 0.50):
        for beta in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
            add_candidate(candidates, make_singular_shape(N, alpha, beta, 0), max_candidates)
            add_candidate(candidates, make_singular_shape(N, alpha, beta, 1), max_candidates)

    # Exponential-polynomial inits.
    theta_pool = []
    theta_max = 8

    def consider_theta(theta, score):
        nonlocal_support = []  # placeholder to avoid accidental closure? This line avoids syntax issues? Not allowed maybe. Use no nested.
```
